### User · 2026-10-10 06:21:17

Background (from the session that started this one):
Small Talk is a press-on nail brand (Shopify store 0nev20-vn.myshopify.com, Basic plan, INR) for 18–24 urban desi women. "the talks" is the brand's community space: candid, small, Hinglish conversations. A multi-session team rebuilt the store on theme 186651934933 "Small Talk — Claude build (Horizon)" (UNPUBLISHED; preview https://0nev20-vn.myshopify.com/?preview_theme_id=186651934933). An Opus orchestrator session (st-orchestrator, local_e4896004-68cd-4d55-9d37-ab925783b63b) owns the checklist C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\Small-Talk-Claude-Implementation-Checklist.md and grants the single theme-write lock; st-foundation builds theme code, st-copy owns shopper-visible wording, st-fable does final visual acceptance against the boards. Current talks implementation: blog "the-talks" with 9 seed articles posted by Small Talk, replies via Shopify's native blog comment form (which forces name + email), and a "Start a talk" CONTACT form that only emails the team, so nothing a shopper writes ever appears. The owner tried it and saw nothing appear. Design boards: C:\Users\SSTS\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-SSTS-Desktop-Riya\01e61cb4-b351-4d98-a557-f8818be4c4e7\images\4.jpg (frames "the talks" and "Conversation"); measured spec in C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\spec\talks.md and spec\conversation.md; tokens in spec\tokens.css. Never touch any theme other than 186651934933; the live theme is 186573160661 and must not be edited or published over.

Task:
Build "the talks" into a real, working, anonymous thread feature on the Small Talk store. Owner's requirements, verbatim intent:
- Candid, minimal, clear UI with Reddit-style thread visualisation, suited to an anonymous gossip channel.
- No email, no name, no login required to post. To start a talk the shopper only picks a topic (or types her own), writes her talk inside the thread interface, and taps once to publish. It must appear immediately in the talks list.
- Replies work the same way: write inside the thread, one tap, appears immediately.
- Keep the board look (spec\talks.md, spec\conversation.md, tokens.css): set-nail avatars, topic chips, burgundy pills, warm ivory ground.

Technical reality you must solve (a Liquid theme alone cannot create content from the storefront):
1. Data: store threads and replies as Shopify metaobjects (e.g. talk_thread: topic, body, alias, set_avatar, status, report_count; talk_reply: thread reference, body, alias, status, report_count), with storefront read access, so the theme renders them in Liquid (paginated, newest first, topic filter) and optionally a thread page via metaobject templates. Migrate the 9 existing seed articles into threads marked as posted by "Small Talk" (not presented as customers). Creating these metaobject definitions and entries is authorised by the owner for this feature; don't change products, prices, policies or other shared data.
2. Write path: a small serverless backend that receives a post from the storefront and creates the metaobject entry with the Admin API. Check current shopify.dev guidance (search_docs_chunks) for the right approach today: an app with an App Proxy at /apps/talks (same domain, HMAC-signed, passes logged_in_customer_id) is preferred; a standalone function with CORS locked to the store domain is the fallback. Hosting: the Vercel connector is available in this environment; there is no Node or Shopify CLI on this machine. Any secret (Admin API token, app secret) must be created and pasted by the OWNER, never by you: write exact step-by-step admin instructions for those steps and stop at them. Never put a token in theme code or in the repo.
3. Safety for an anonymous channel (non-negotiable, but invisible to honest users): auto-generated playful alias per device (localStorage) that she can edit, no account needed; rate limit per IP/device; honeypot; server-side filter that blocks phone numbers, emails, @handles, URLs and slurs, and holds posts that look like they name or target a real person for review; a "report" link on every thread and reply, auto-hiding after 3 reports; owner can hide/delete from Shopify admin (metaobject status). Add short community rules and a grievance/contact line under the composer (wording via st-copy, below).
4. Optimistic UI: the post appears instantly in the list, then confirms; failed posts show a clear retry. No page reload needed. Works without login; if a customer is logged in, nothing extra is required.

Process:
- Read the checklist RESUME HERE block, spec\talks.md, spec\conversation.md, spec\tokens.css, and the current theme sections for the talks (theme-build\src\sections and snippets with "talk" in the name; build-log-foundation.md).
- Write a short design note first (C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\talks\DESIGN.md): data model, endpoints, moderation, UI states (empty, posting, posted, failed, reported/hidden), and the owner's admin steps. Send it to st-orchestrator (SendMessage to local_e4896004-68cd-4d55-9d37-ab925783b63b) and proceed unless it objects.
- Build the backend in C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\talks\ and deploy it. Theme changes: ask st-orchestrator for the theme-write lock, write only the talks files, verify on the preview, release the lock.
- Shopper-visible wording (composer placeholder, topic chips, button labels, rules, errors, success): request it from st-copy via st-orchestrator; use clear placeholders until it arrives.
- Verify end to end on the preview at 390 and 1280: post a talk with no login and no email, see it appear, reply, report, filter by topic, blocked-content message, rate-limit message. Then delete your test posts.
- Report to st-orchestrator when done, with what the owner must do (admin steps) and screenshots in C:\Users\SSTS\Desktop\Riya\qa\talks\.

1	// the talks: relative times (TLK-009). Each <time data-st-ago="<unix seconds>"> renders "9 Oct" from Liquid; under
2	// 7 days old it becomes "just now", "n min ago", "nh ago" or "nd ago". The patterns come from locale through data
3	// attributes on [data-st-ago-strings], so no copy lives here.
4	
5	const strings = /** @type {HTMLElement | null} */ (document.querySelector('[data-st-ago-strings]'))?.dataset ?? {};
6	
7	/** @param {string | undefined} pattern @param {number} n */
8	const fill = (pattern, n) => (pattern ? pattern.replace('__n__', String(n)) : null);
9	
10	for (const el of /** @type {NodeListOf<HTMLTimeElement>} */ (document.querySelectorAll('time[data-st-ago]'))) {
11	  const at = Number(el.dataset.stAgo) * 1000;
12	  if (!at) continue;
13	  const minutes = Math.floor((Date.now() - at) / 60000);
14	  if (minutes < 0) continue;
15	  let text = null;
16	  if (minutes < 1) text = strings.agoNow ?? null;
17	  else if (minutes < 60) text = fill(strings.agoMinutes, minutes);
18	  else if (minutes < 1440) text = fill(strings.agoHours, Math.floor(minutes / 60));
19	  else if (minutes < 10080) text = fill(strings.agoDays, Math.floor(minutes / 1440));
20	  if (text) el.textContent = text;
21	}
22

"""stage_put.py <xgoogdate> <local> <uuid/filename> <signature> [mime]  -> PUT + verify md5 via public GET"""
import sys, subprocess, hashlib
date, local, path, sig = sys.argv[1:5]; mime = sys.argv[5] if len(sys.argv) > 5 else 'text/plain'
B = "https://shopify-staged-uploads.storage.googleapis.com/tmp/83023397077/files/"
Q = ("?X-Goog-Algorithm=GOOG4-RSA-SHA256&X-Goog-Credential=merchant-assets%40shopify-tiers.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
     "%2F20261009%2Fauto%2Fstorage%2Fgoog4_request&X-Goog-Date=" + date + "&X-Goog-Expires=604800&X-Goog-SignedHeaders=host&X-Goog-Signature=" + sig)
put = subprocess.run(["curl", "-s", "-o", "/dev/null", "-w", "%{http_code}", "-X", "PUT", "-H", "Content-Type: " + mime, "--data-binary", "@" + local, B + path + Q], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
body = subprocess.run(["curl", "-s", B + path], capture_output=True).stdout
ok = hashlib.md5(body).hexdigest() == hashlib.md5(open(local, 'rb').read()).hexdigest()
print(put, 'md5-ok' if ok else 'MD5-MISMATCH', B + path)

def _http(url: str, data: bytes, headers: dict, timeout: float = 12.0) -> tuple[int, dict]:
    req = Request(url, data=data, headers=headers, method='POST')
    try:
        with urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as res:
            return res.status, json.loads(res.read() or b'{}')
    except HTTPError as e:
        try:

<details><summary>result</summary>

```
"""stage_batch.py <targets.txt>: each line "local_path<TAB>staged_url<TAB>mime". PUTs the file to the signed staged URL,
re-downloads the public copy and checks its md5. Prints one line per file and the resourceUrl to use in themeFilesUpsert."""
import sys, subprocess, hashlib

ok = True
for line in open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8'):
    line = line.strip()
    if not line:
        continue
    local, url, mime = line.split('\t')
    base = url.split('?')[0]
    put = subprocess.run(['curl', '-s', '-o', '/dev/null', '-w', '%{http_code}', '-X', 'PUT', '-H', 'Content-Type: ' + mime,
                          '--data-binary', '@' + local, url], capture_output=True, text=True).stdout
    body = subprocess.run(['curl', '-s', base], capture_output=True).stdout
    same = hashlib.md5(body).hexdigest() == hashlib.md5(open(local, 'rb').read()).hexdigest()
    ok &= put == '200' and same
    print(put, 'md5-ok' if same else 'MD5-MISMATCH', local, base)
print('ALL OK' if ok else 'PROBLEMS')

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 07:11:48

The talks are rebuilt as an anonymous thread board, but nobody can post yet. The backend isn't deployed, because the Vercel (v0) connector needs you to reconnect it. So the preview currently shows "Posting opens soon. Read along for now." wherever the posting box will go, as the orchestrator asked. I've handed the theme lock back.

**What's done:**
- **Data:** the talks now live in Shopify metaobjects (threads, replies, topics, plus two private types for settings and rate-limit records). The 9 seed talks are migrated with their original URLs, posted as "small talk". Nothing else in the store was touched.
- **Theme (preview only):** the talks hub keeps the board look and has topic chips with their own pages and a one-tap composer as the first row. Threads show replies Reddit-style, one level deep. Old talk links forward to the new thread pages. There are no name, email or login fields anywhere.
- **Backend:** written and tested locally (28 tests pass), not yet live. It covers:
  - rate limits per device and network;
  - blocking phone numbers, emails, UPI IDs, @handles, links and slurs;
  - holding posts that might name a real person for a quick check;
  - auto-hiding anything after 3 reports.

**Testing:** all pages render with no errors at 390 and 1280. With posting temporarily switched on and the backend faked in the browser, every state worked (nothing was sent to Shopify):
- her talk appears straight away (about 0.1 s);
- held, failed with Retry, slow-down and blocked messages show correctly;
- nested replies and Report work.

The real end-to-end test hasn't run yet, so there are no test posts to delete. Screenshots are in `qa\talks\`.

**Speed, as you asked:** pages cost the same as the old blog. The backend only runs when someone posts, replies or reports. One small script loads, and only on talks pages.

**Still open:**
1. **You:** reconnect v0 in claude.ai → Settings → Connectors → v0, then follow `talks\OWNER-SETUP.md` (about 15 minutes). You create a small private Shopify app and paste its two credentials into Vercel yourself; they never pass through me.
2. **Me, after that:** deploy the backend, switch posting on, run the real test (post, reply, report, topic filter, blocked and slow-down messages), and delete the test posts.
3. **Shopper-facing text:** all of it is placeholder until st-copy finishes `talks\COPY-REQUEST.md`.
4. **Old articles:** I've proposed to the orchestrator that the 9 old blog articles be unpublished and redirected to the new pages.
5. **Network limits:** these depend on Vercel passing the shopper's network address through Shopify's proxy, which I'll check after deploying. If it doesn't, the per-device and store-wide limits still apply.

The full hand-over note is `st-talks-live.md` in the shared notes folder.

**Privacy:**

- The talks never ask for or store a name, email, phone number or account.
  - A signed-in customer is not linked: the post only notes "signed-in customer", never the ID.
- Raw IP addresses are never stored or logged.
  - Network and device are kept as salted one-way HMAC keys (24 hex characters) in the private `talk_signal` type
    (storefront NONE).
  - These are used only for rate limits, duplicate checks and one-report-per-person.
  - They are deleted automatically after about 7 days.
- Function logs record action, result and timing only: no text, no IP.
- The alias, avatar and device ID live in the shopper's own browser (localStorage).
- A grievance/takedown line (store contact email) sits under every composer and reply box, and the house rules
  explain Report.

**Posting live switch:**

- This is a section setting ("Posting live", in 3 templates).
- While the backend isn't configured it stays off, and pages show "Posting opens soon. Read along for now." instead
  of a box that can't post (orchestrator condition).

**Copy:**

- Strings live in the locale (`small_talk.talks.live.*`, the convention the register maps to), not in section
  settings.
- The slots are in COPY-REQUEST.md.

Old codes are deleted after 7 days, on every post and by a daily clean-up job, so the "delete it within 30 days" line on the site holds even when nobody posts.
- **Filters:** blocking contact details, links and slurs, and holding posts that may name a real person, are always on in code. You can only make them stricter.

Use the Python runtime to run ASGI (Asynchronous Server Gateway Interface) and
WSGI (Web Server Gateway Interface) applications on Vercel. Vercel detects
supported Python frameworks and runs your application as Vercel Functions.

# Python Functions in the /api Directory

Vercel supports existing projects that define file-based Python functions in
an `/api` directory. File-based Python functions map `.py` files to routes and
load ASGI applications, WSGI applications, or `BaseHTTPRequestHandler`
subclasses.

### User · 2026-10-10 07:56:25

<cross-session-message from="local_e4896004-68cd-4d55-9d37-ab925783b63b" name="Small Talk — Orchestrator">
Orchestrator: there's one bug left in the proxy-mode IP path. Please fix it BEFORE the owner clicks Publish, so it all goes out in one publish.

talks/backend/app.py:573 runs `burst_guard(f'{kind}:{ip_key}', limit=6)`. Behind the proxy, ip_key is '', so the key becomes 'post:' / 'reply:' / 'report:'. Those strings are non-empty, so burst_guard's `if not key: return` never fires.
Result: every shopper shares one bucket. The 7th post per minute from anyone on a warm instance gets rate_limited. The same thing happens at :572 if device_key is ever ''.

Fix:
- only call each guard when its key exists: `if device_key: burst_guard(f'{kind}:{device_key}')` and `if ip_key: burst_guard(f'{kind}:{ip_key}', limit=6)`;
- reject or limit requests with no device id explicitly, rather than bucketing them together;
- add a test: 8 posts from 8 different devices within 60 s behind the proxy all succeed, and 5 from one device are throttled.

Then update the v0 chat and tell the parent/owner that the Publish click covers this build. Send me the new build hash, which I'll verify on /api/talks after publish.

The rest checks out against the code: handle_request sets ctx ip=None for signed proxy requests (line ~875), keyed('') returns '', record_signal stores ip_key None, and the report path is the same.
</cross-session-message>

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 07:57:14

The backend needed no new code this round, and the only Publish click you need is the one in setup step 5.

- **The bug the orchestrator flagged** (every shopper sharing one rate-limit counter behind Shopify's proxy) is already fixed in build `c91d3d11afc1`, which is the version waiting in v0. Its message crossed with my fix.
- **Its second concern can't happen.** A post, reply or report with a missing or malformed device ID is refused before it reaches any rate limit (`app.py` lines 671–673 and 791–793), so shoppers can never end up sharing an empty bucket. Adding an extra guard would mean another full re-upload for no change in behaviour, so I left the code as it is.
- **I added the tests the orchestrator asked for:**
  - 8 different phones posting within a minute all succeed.
  - 5 posts from one phone go 3 through, then 2 refused.
  - A missing device ID is rejected.

  All 37 tests pass.

The live site still runs an earlier build. It has the old bug, but nothing can post yet, so it's harmless.

What's left for you, in `talks\OWNER-SETUP.md`:

1. Once Shopify's email verification clears, do steps 1–4 (create the app, the app proxy, install it, copy the credentials).
2. In step 5, add the four settings in Vercel, then press **Publish in v0** — not Vercel's Redeploy button, which rebuilds the old code. The earlier extra Publish I asked for is no longer needed.
3. Check that https://small-talk-talks-backend.vercel.app/api/talks shows `"configured": true` and build `c91d3d11afc1`.

After that I'll confirm on the store that no shopper IP reaches the backend, then do the posting-on and real-test steps once the orchestrator grants the theme lock.

          break
        endif

  assign who = alias | default: article.author | strip
  assign initial = who | remove_fi

"""set_live.py on|off [root] : flips the talks posting switch, the single value in snippets/st-talk-live.liquid that the
composer, reply box, Report, the pre-launch notice (TLK-153) and house rule 5 (TLK-075 / TLK-154) all read.
Run from theme-build/src (default root '.'); upload only snippets/st-talk-live.liquid afterwards, under the lock."""
import re
import sys

value = sys.argv[1]
assert value in ('on', 'off'), 'use on or off'
root = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else '.'
path = f'{root}/snippets/st-talk-live.liquid'
s = open(path, encoding='utf-8').read()
s2, n = re.subn(r"assign st_talks_posting = '(on|off)'", f"assign st_talks_posting = '{value}'", s)
assert n == 1, 'switch line not found'
open(path, 'w', encoding='utf-8', newline='\n').write(s2)
print(path, '->', value)
{% doc %}
  The talks posting switch: the ONE value that the composer, the reply box, Report, the pre-launch notice (TLK-153)
  and house rule 5 (TLK-075 live / TLK-154 pre-launch) all read, so they can never disagree. Outputs "on" or "off".
  Turn it on only after the talks backend is set up and verified end to end (talks/OWNER-SETUP.md);
  talks/set_live.py flips it. The backend's own kill switch after launch is Talks settings → Posting open (TLK-130).

  @example
  {%- capture live_flag -%}{%- render 'st-talk-live' -%}{%- endcapture -%}
  {%- liquid
    assign live = false
    if live_flag == 'on'
      assign live = true
    endif
  -%}
{% enddoc %}
{%- assign st_talks_posting = 'off' -%}

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 09:04:08

I've staged the switch of the talks avatars to the new high-definition (HD) nail images, but nothing is written to the theme yet. It waits for the owner's setup and a fresh write lock from the orchestrator. One thing needs your attention: this makes the talks pages heavier the first time someone visits.

**Extra download on a first visit (avatar images only):**

| Page | Before | After | Extra |
|---|---|---|---|
| Talks hub | 180 KB | 485 KB | +305 KB |
| Topic page | about 70 KB | about 177 KB | about +107 KB |
| Thread page | 23 KB | 60 KB | +37 KB |

- **What it means in practice:** the text and layout don't wait for these images; they fill in alongside. On a slow phone connection they do compete for bandwidth.
- **Visitors who have seen the homepage:** they already have these exact images cached, so it costs them nothing. Keeping the old images would actually make those visitors download both sets.
- **No easy way to defer them:** one CSS approach doesn't delay them at all (I measured it). Native lazy-loading would save little, because the browser starts loading most of the hub anyway.

I've passed these numbers to the orchestrator and said I'll install as st-fable ruled unless they want otherwise.

**What's staged:**
- **The change:** three small files.
  - The avatar file now uses the homepage hero's HD nail images, one per nail set across the whole site.
  - The settings file does the same for a just-posted message.
  - The posting switch is flipped to on.
- **How it looks:** following st-fable's rule, each nail is 1.058× larger, anchored at the base of the nail. Rotation and position are unchanged.
- **Checked on the preview without writing to the theme:** no nail tip is clipped by the circle on the hub or a thread. The tip now sits about 5 px higher, about 7 px from the edge instead of 12.
- **Where the old images come from:** today every request for the old nail images on the talks pages comes from these avatars, so this swap removes all of them.
- **Install order:** the two avatar files first, with posting still off. Then I check that no old image is requested and take phone-width screenshots, switch posting on, run the real end-to-end test, and delete the test posts.

The install plan is `talks/staged/posting-on/INSTALL.md`, and the side-by-side before/after avatar images are in `qa/talks/` (`hd-avatar-probe-*.png`).

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 09:25:49

I've staged the accessibility fix for the example conversations on /pages/the-talks; nothing is written to the theme yet. Before this change, axe reports 3 serious "listitem" errors on that page. With it, there are none, and the page looks exactly the same. I've sent this to the orchestrator.

- **Cause:** each example card was coded as a list item, but Shopify wraps every block in its own container, so the cards could never sit directly inside their list.
- **Fix:** each card becomes a standalone article and the list becomes a plain container. The class names stay the same and no styling depends on the old tags, so nothing shifts visually.

  | File | Change | md5 |
  |---|---|---|
  | `blocks/st-talk-example.liquid` | list item → article | 73fcea21 → 44941c76 |
  | `blocks/st-talk-examples.liquid` | list → plain container | fef12c7b → b5162be3 |

- **How I checked:** I applied the same change to the page in the browser on the preview theme and ran axe's two list checks, at 390 and 1280. Screenshots before and after match pixel for pixel at both widths.
- **Also uses these blocks:** the `page.desi-talks` template, but no page uses it (/pages/desi-talks returns "not found").

The posting-ON window now covers 5 files: the 3 snippets plus these 2 blocks. The theme's copies of all 5 still match my last upload. The two blocks go up together with the avatar files while posting is still off, and the axe check on /pages/the-talks will be in that window's report. The plan is in `talks/staged/posting-on/INSTALL.md`.

  /* Composer: board talk-row language (avatar disc + white pill field), the reply box's #a80f2a action colour and

Measured on the preview with the staged change applied in the browser, at 1280×720, 1366×768, 1440×800 and 1536×703:
- **Wider content:** the main column is now 751–790 px wide, up from 600 px on the hub and 386 px on a thread.
- **First screen:** the heading, the first talk and the right-hand column are all visible together.
- **No sideways scroll.**
- **Sticky card:** the right-hand "Start a talk" card stays just below the header while you scroll, and stops above the house rules without overlapping them.
- **Tap targets:** none in the right-hand column or the house rules is smaller than 44 px.
- **Phones untouched:** full-page screenshots at 390×844, 990 and 1023 are pixel-identical to today. One run showed a single differing pixel on the hub at 1023; the re-runs were identical, so it was render noise.

**What the layout is:**
- **Hub and topic pages:** the title block stays centred across the top. Below it, the topic chips and the list of talks take the left two-thirds, with talk titles at 24 px. On the right, the "Start a talk" card stays put while you scroll, and the house rules close that column.
- **Thread page:** the thread and its replies are on the left, with the title at 32 px, text at 18 px and the avatar at 96 px. The "Start a talk" panel is on the right and stays put, with the house rules below it.
- **How it's built:**
  - The two-column grid applies only to talks pages. The news blog and article pages don't change.
  - It uses st-fable's shared page width and column gap.
  - The only markup change is a plain wrapper around the hub's list, which renders identically on phones.

Three places where I didn't follow the spec exactly; st-fable should confirm or overrule:
1. **No second "Start a talk" panel on the hub.** I moved the hub's existing post box to the right column instead. A second panel would mean two post boxes on one page once posting opens. While posting is off, the right column shows the "Posting isn't open yet" note, which the spec placed on the left.
2. **Only the "Start a talk" card stays put, not the house rules.** The rules are 657 px tall, but only 579–676 px fits under the header at those screen sizes. If they stayed put, the helpline line at the bottom would be hidden.
3. **Some headings keep their current size:** the hub title (95 px), the house rules heading and the "Start a talk" heading. The sizes the spec states explicitly are all applied.

The four section files are staged in `talks/staged/desktop/src/sections/`. The theme's current copies still match my last upload, and the upload order and post-install checks are in `talks/staged/desktop/INSTALL.md`.

I couldn't check the open post box in the right column, because it only appears once posting is on. I'll check it at 1280 in the posting-ON window.

The live theme is now 186695155925 (the owner published again at 14:12Z). I've corrected the stale ID in my notes and the posting-ON plan, and replaced my duplicate memory note with a link to the existing one.

- **Kept the home page light:** loading the whole talks script there would add about 33 KB (9.5 KB compressed). Instead it's a new 1.3 KB file, `assets/st-talk-ago.js`.

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 19:38:19

I've made a first draft of the simpler, Reddit-style talks design: side-by-side screenshots of the current site and the proposal, plus a written spec. Nothing on the theme changed. I haven't sent it to you yet because st-fable hasn't given its design direction and st-copy hasn't written the final wording; every line of text in the renders is a placeholder marked DRAFT.

The comparison sheets cover the hub, the composer, a thread and the home-page teaser, each at phone (390×844) and laptop (1536×703) size. There are also proposal-only renders of the phone hub before posting opens and of a topic with no talks yet.

What the proposal changes:
- **Starting a talk:** a "spill your tea…" bar sits at the top of the talks page. One tap opens a short form: pick a topic, write (the first line becomes the headline), with two tappable starter lines per topic, posting under a shuffleable alias, then "post it".
- **The talks list:** each talk is a compact card showing the avatar, alias, time ("1d"), a topic tag, the title, a line or two of text and the reply count. The page title "the talks" uses the handwritten font already on the site, replacing the huge serif heading.
- **Threads:** the original post sits in a card. Replies show one indented level under the reply they answer, which is the most nesting the backend stores. Phones get a reply bar fixed at the bottom of the screen.
- **Laptops:** three columns, Reddit-style: topics on the left, the talks in the middle, "how it works" on the right.
- **Reading and density:** text goes up from 13–15 px to 16–19 px. A phone screen shows 3 talks instead of 1, and a large monitor shows 5 instead of 3.
- **Speed:** only fonts the site already loads.
- **Unchanged:** the posting backend, anonymity, moderation and the spam limits.

A few things in the renders aren't real data. The three replies on the sample thread and the "3 replies" on the first card are made up, so you can see how nesting and counts look; all nine talks really have 0 replies. The current composer and reply box are shown as they'll look once posting opens, because posting is still off today.

Two things are still pending:
- **Final sheets:** once I hear back from st-fable and st-copy, I'll redo the renders and send the final sheets to the orchestrator to bring to you.
- **Talks app:** once you've installed the app, set the Vercel variables and published from v0, I'll check that the backend address reports build `c91d3d11afc1` and `"configured": true`.

Start with `qa/talks-ux/README.md`; the comparison sheets are in `qa/talks-ux/renders/` (named `compare-*`).

### Assistant · 2026-10-10 20:00:42

The talks backend is safe while posting is closed, and the final design screenshots (v1) are ready for st-fable's review before they go to you. Nothing on the theme or in store data changed in this stretch.

**Backend checks (read-only, plus empty test requests that couldn't create anything):**
- **App setup:** confirmed. The backend reports build `c91d3d11afc1` with `"configured": true`, requests from your store reach it through Shopify's proxy, and the backend itself reads posting as off.
- **Posts and replies are blocked on the server**, not just hidden in the page:
  - The off switch is checked first, before any validation or saving. I confirmed this with deliberately empty requests, which were refused as "posting closed".
  - Sending directly to the Vercel address, bypassing the store, is refused too.
- **Reading** never goes through the backend, so it keeps working while posting is off. I built it that way.
- **Reports** still work while posting is paused, so people can flag harmful content during a pause. Today they can't change anything, because all 9 talks are brand posts, which reports skip.
- **Two hardening items** for the next backend update, which needs your OK and your Publish click in v0:
  - Remove the IP-diagnostic address. It stores and logs nothing, but it's no longer needed.
  - Treat a missing posting setting as "closed". Today a missing setting means open.
- **Posting-ON plan:** it now records the switch's location and the order: turn posting on only for the end-to-end test, keep it on only if the test passes, turn it straight back off if anything fails.

**Topics, your decision.** Every screenshot uses st-copy's proposed five topics: plot twists, tiny wins, second opinions, just venting, nail talk. A separate image compares them with today's four, with a pro and con for each.
- The backend files every "your own" topic post under "something else". So "retiring" it can only mean hiding it from the topic list. Deleting it would break "your own" posts.
- No talks are filed under "something else" today, so nothing would need to move.

**Design v1** follows st-fable's direction and st-copy's final wording:
- Handwritten font only for "the talks"; DM Sans everywhere else.
- Flat list rows with dividers.
- A red "spill your tea" button fixed at the bottom on phones.
- A guided composer: bottom sheet on phones, side panel on desktop.
- Threads with one indented reply level.
- A three-column desktop layout.

The list is much denser:

| Screen | Talks in the first screen (today → proposed) | Screens to scroll all 9 talks (today → proposed) |
|---|---|---|
| Phone (390) | 0 → 4 | 2.51 → 1.16 |
| Laptop (1536) | 1 → 4 | 2.25 → 1.24 |
| Large monitor (1920) | 3 → 8 | 1.47 → 0.80 |

Two points need st-fable's call:
- **Row height:** rows are 94–97 px against its target of about 84–92. Hitting 84 on desktop would mean dropping the one-line preview text.
- **Desktop composer:** in the side panel it's taller than a 1536×703 screen, so the page scrolls to reach "post it". Opening it at the top of the list instead would fit on one screen.

Two placeholders in the composer (the headline and details boxes) are still drafts; I've asked st-copy for the final words. Once st-fable accepts and you approve, the design and posting-ON go in one combined window, followed by the real end-to-end test.

Everything is in `qa/talks-ux/`: start with `README.md`, and the side-by-side comparisons are `renders/compare-*`.
